\chapter{Translation-Gated Structural-Order Boundary for $B^*_{Q6}$ Residual Coordinates}
\label{ch:q6-translation-gated-structural-order-cross-layer}
\origin{ai}

\subsection{Finite Carrier and Readout Discipline}
\label{sec:q6-translation-gated-structural-order-carrier}

\paragraph{Carrier.}
The object considered in this chapter is a finite CDS-indexed surface whose rows are encoded proteins and whose coordinate columns are the nine $B^*_{Q6}$ synonymous residual coordinates
$K_{AAA}$, $Arg_{AGR}$, $Ile_{AUA}$, $Leu_{CUN\,vs\,UUR}$, $Leu_{UUA\,vs\,UUG}$, $Ser_{UCR\,vs\,AGY}$, $Ser_{UCA\,vs\,UCG}$, $Thr_{ACR\,vs\,ACY}$, and $f3_{stress}$.  Each row carries codon-derived residual coordinates, an organism assignment, CDS length, amino-acid composition, GC3, an $M$-density baseline, and, where available, a protein-level structural-order readout.  The chapter does not begin from a folded-structure claim.  It begins from a finite residual-coordinate panel and asks whether a bounded readout can be associated with that panel after the relevant composition and sequence controls have been removed.

\paragraph{Gated claim form.}
The admissible chain is
$$
\begin{aligned}
q_i \longrightarrow T_j \longrightarrow SO,
\end{aligned}
$$
where $q_i$ is one of the nine residual coordinates, $T_j$ is a translation-layer readout, and $SO$ is a structural-order readback.  The order of this chain matters.  A structural-order association is not licensed directly from codon usage, from the residual geometry, from $M$-density, or from amino-acid composition.  A coordinate is allowed to enter the structural-order test only after the same coordinate has a translation-readout route and after the structural readout is conditioned on translation and protein-abundance baselines when those contacts are available.  If no coordinate survives the translation-readout gate, the structural-order table is empty by construction.

\paragraph{Observed finite table.}
The finite row table presently attached to this claim uses AlphaFold mean pLDDT on the scale $0$ to $100$ as a predicted-structure-confidence readout.  This is a bounded computational readout, not an experimental folding measurement.  In Escherichia coli K12 MG1655, $3739$ codon rows were joined to the structural-order table, $3736$ matched proteins were used, and the join hit rate was $0.999198$.  In Saccharomyces cerevisiae, $5815$ codon rows were joined, $5804$ matched proteins were used, and the join hit rate was $0.998108$.  Thus the two finite surfaces are nearly complete with respect to their available protein matches, but this high matching rate is an identifier-coverage fact rather than a biological mechanism.

\subsection{Control Geometry and Rank Checks}
\label{sec:q6-translation-gated-structural-order-controls}

\paragraph{Control set.}
The controlled regression surface uses an intercept, $\log(\mathrm{cds\_len\_nt})$, the twenty amino-acid composition proportions $A,C,D,E,F,G,H,I,K,L,M,N,P,Q,R,S,T,V,W,Y$, GC3 computed from real CDS codon counts, and an $M$-density baseline defined as the fraction of sense codons on the union support of the nine projected $B^*_{Q6}$ vectors.  The amino-acid composition controls are essential: without them, a codon-coordinate association with pLDDT could merely restate a protein-composition association.

\paragraph{Rank diagnostics.}
For Escherichia coli K12 MG1655, the controlled design used $20$ amino-acid composition controls and $24$ total control columns.  The rank of $Z$ was $23$, the residualized coordinate rank $\operatorname{rank}(\widetilde X)$ was $9$, the residual degrees of freedom after $Z$ were $3713$, the explained energy was $4178.935925251874$, and the residual readout energy was $136607.52873622958$.  For Saccharomyces cerevisiae, the corresponding values were $20$ amino-acid composition controls, $24$ total control columns, $\operatorname{rank}(Z)=23$, $\operatorname{rank}(\widetilde X)=9$, residual degrees of freedom $5781$, explained energy $44421.14342043593$, and residual readout energy $620506.1490869389$.  Both organisms are marked rank-sufficient for the nine-coordinate test, with a degeneracy threshold $0.999999$.

\paragraph{Association statistic.}
The structural-order association is reported as a controlled $R^2_{SO}$ surface after the declared controls.  The positive partial-$R^2$ threshold recorded for the run is $10^{-12}$.  The finite outcome is
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^2_{SO}(\mathrm{E.\ coli\ K12\ MG1655}) &= 0.03059081709413562,\\
R^2_{SO}(\mathrm{S.\ cerevisiae}) &= 0.0715885627979685.
\end{aligned}
$$
In both organisms the recorded surviving-coordinate count is $9$.  These numbers show finite cross-sectional association between the residual-coordinate panel and predicted structure confidence under the listed controls.  They do not show causality, folding kinetics, physical admissibility, molecular function, localization, abundance, phenotype, or a universal law.

\subsection{Local Interpretation of the Structural-Order Readback}
\label{sec:q6-translation-gated-structural-order-interpretation}

\paragraph{What the positive rows mean.}
The finite result says that the nine-coordinate residualized codon panel has nonzero controlled association with AlphaFold mean pLDDT in the two attached organism tables.  In Escherichia coli the effect is modest, with $R^2_{SO}=0.03059081709413562$; in Saccharomyces cerevisiae the association is larger but still bounded, with $R^2_{SO}=0.0715885627979685$.  The row counts are high enough to make the design rank-sufficient in the recorded calculation, and the residualized coordinate rank remains the full coordinate count $9$ in both organisms.  The correct reading is therefore a finite, organism-scoped, controlled association with predicted structure confidence.

\paragraph{What the positive rows do not mean.}
AlphaFold mean pLDDT is a predicted-confidence field.  It is not an experimental structure-order assay, not a folded-state measurement, not a protein-function assay, not a protein-abundance measurement, not a localization readout, not a pathway readout, and not a perturbation result.  A positive controlled association with pLDDT does not identify a translation-mediated mechanism.  It also does not prove that synonymous residual coordinates alter folding, stability, physical admissibility, function, or phenotype.  Those claims require their own layer-matched contacts and their own finite statistics.

\paragraph{Null boundary.}
The structural-order entry is null whenever the required upstream and layer-matched contacts are absent.  Translation readouts such as tAI, stAI, ribosome occupancy, translation efficiency, or protein abundance cannot be silently reinterpreted as folded-structure measurements.  Protein abundance cannot stand in for structure.  mRNA abundance is a transcription-layer control, not a translation-output measurement.  Codon-usage tables and $B^*_{Q6}$ residual coordinates describe sequence-side variation; they do not by themselves observe ribosome motion, protein folding, physical feasibility, molecular function, organismal phenotype, or biological universality.

\subsection{Finite Decision Record}
\label{sec:q6-translation-gated-structural-order-decision}

\paragraph{Decision.}
The claim is ready as a bounded BioReality chapter because the attached run supplies a finite coordinate list, organism-specific row counts, high structural-order join rates, explicit control columns, rank diagnostics, residual degrees of freedom, a positive-threshold convention, and two controlled $R^2_{SO}$ values.  The chapter must remain scoped to the following statement: per-protein $B^*_{Q6}$ codon-usage residual coordinates are associated with AlphaFold mean pLDDT predicted-confidence values in two organism-matched finite tables after length, twenty amino-acid composition proportions, GC3, and $M$-density controls.

\paragraph{Local stop rule.}
The stop rule is equally important.  No translation realization follows unless a translation contact supplies a tested readout under the declared controls.  No folding, physical admissibility, function, mechanism, phenotype, or universality follows unless a separate contact at that layer is attached and evaluated.  The present finite table can support only a controlled association with predicted structure confidence; it cannot promote the residual-coordinate panel into a general biological mechanism.

\origin{ai}

\subsection{Direct Protein-Omics Survival With a Translation Boundary}
\label{sec:q-six-direct-protein-omics-survival-translation-boundary}

\paragraph{Finite row object.}
The record considered here is a finite coding-sequence table whose rows are organism-matched CDS entries and whose columns separate three kinds of quantities. The first block contains $B^{\ast}_{Q6}$ synonymous residual coordinates after the amino-acid quotient. The computed coordinate list is
$$
\begin{aligned}
R_{Q6} = \{&K\_AAA,\ Arg\_AGR,\ Ile\_AUA,\ Leu\_CUN\_vs\_UUR,\ Leu\_UUA\_vs\_UUG,\\
&Ser\_UCR\_vs\_AGY,\ Ser\_UCA\_vs\_UCG,\ Thr\_ACR\_vs\_ACY,\ f3\_stress\}.
\end{aligned}
$$
Thus the tested residual block has $9$ coordinates. The second block is reserved for translation-layer measurements or bounded translation proxies. The third block contains protein-omics readouts. The present positive computation is in the direct $Q \to P$ block: the readout is $\log_{10}(\mathrm{abundance\_ppm})$ from measured PAXdb protein abundance joined to real CDS codon counts. The required boundary is that this direct block is not allowed to stand in for a translation-rate, ribosome-occupancy, mediation, folding, function, phenotype, physical-admissibility, or universal-law statement.

\paragraph{Sequence-to-protein joins.}
The protein-abundance computation used three organism-specific finite joins. For \emph{Escherichia coli} K-12 MG1655, $3739$ proteins were joined, with hit rate $0.9978649586335735$. For \emph{Mycobacterium smegmatis} str. mc2 155, $4129$ proteins were joined, with hit rate $1.0$. For \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}, $5815$ proteins were joined, with hit rate $0.9156038419146592$. These row counts are large enough for the direct per-CDS residual regressions recorded below, but they do not by themselves supply a measured translation mediator. They only establish that protein-abundance rows were available at the same organismal level as the CDS codon-count rows.

\paragraph{Residualization controls.}
For each organism, the protein-omics readout was residualized against the declared baseline controls before the $B^{\ast}_{Q6}$ block was tested. The control set includes length, amino-acid composition, GC3, and M-density terms; the claim statement additionally requires mRNA and measured translation-readout controls before a mediation interpretation can be made. In the direct protein-abundance computation actually recorded, each organism used $24$ control columns with control rank $23$, and the residualized $Q$ block retained rank $9$. The residual degrees of freedom were $3716$ for \emph{E. coli}, $4106$ for \emph{M. smegmatis}, and $5792$ for yeast. The corresponding residual readout energies were $4020.333671802061$, $5729.194538214237$, and $2958.1865890925337$, while the explained energies under the baseline controls were $432.89050065533684$, $183.85037813566998$, and $882.6526210803958$. The degenerate-$R^2$ cutoff used in these fits was $0.999999$, and all three organism fits were marked rank-sufficient for the $9$-coordinate residual block.

\paragraph{Direct survival scores.}
The direct protein-omics surface is summarized by organism-level $R^2_{QP}$ values. With the positive partial-$R^2$ threshold set at $10^{-12}$, all $9$ residual coordinates survived in each organism-level fit. The recorded values are
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^2_{QP}(\mathrm{E.\ coli\ K12}) &= 0.10767526677985896,\\
R^2_{QP}(\mathrm{M.\ smegmatis\ mc2\ 155}) &= 0.03209009170649934,\\
R^2_{QP}(\mathrm{S.\ cerevisiae}) &= 0.29837624994140827.
\end{aligned}
$$
These numbers support a narrow statement: after the listed composition and sequence-length controls, the $B^{\ast}_{Q6}$ synonymous residual block retains direct predictive association with measured protein abundance in these finite rows. The strongest of the three recorded direct associations is the yeast value, $0.29837624994140827$; the weakest is the \emph{M. smegmatis} value, $0.03209009170649934$. The computation therefore does not collapse to an empty residual block and does not fail the direct $Q \to P$ survival check.

\paragraph{Translation readout boundary.}
A separate modeled translation-contact computation is present, but it is not the same as the measured protein-abundance fit. It used modeled tAI codon weights derived from GtRNAdb tRNA gene copy data and dos Reis wobble $s$-values for $3$ organisms. The sense-codon count was $61$ for \emph{E. coli} K-12, human, and yeast, with zero raw-weight fallback count $0$ in all three modeled tAI readouts. The local GtRNAdb payload included all $20$ standard amino-acid families for each of those organisms: \emph{E. coli} K-12 had $89$ FASTA headers, $87$ matched headers, and $2$ unmatched headers; human had $432$ matched headers out of $432$; yeast had $275$ matched headers out of $275$. The modeled bounded-coupling matrix had $81$ entries above the $0.01$ threshold, with maximum absolute score $3801961.031607896$ and maximum absolute diagonal score $3801961.031607896$. This is useful as bounded translation-layer contact, but the same record explicitly states that $3$ organisms are insufficient for powered survival and that the modeled tAI result is not ribosome-seq or proteomics validation.

\paragraph{Interpretation.}
The finite-row outcome is therefore a direct protein-omics survival result with a mandatory translation boundary. The positive $S^{QP}_{ik}$ surface can be reported as codon-usage residual information that remains predictive of measured protein abundance after the recorded baseline controls. It cannot be reported as a translation mechanism unless a measured translation readout $T_j$ is joined to the same CDS rows and a mediation surface supports $q_i \to T_j \to P_k$. It also cannot be promoted to folded-structure evidence, biochemical-function evidence, localization, post-translational state, protein stability, organismal fitness, physical admissibility, causality, or a general biological rule. A positive direct association in the $Q \to P$ block says that the residual codon topology still carries protein-omics signal; it does not say where the signal enters the biological process.

\paragraph{Null and continuation rule.}
The null condition for this chapter is local and finite: if a matched proteomics readout, a matched translation readout, and the required transcript controls are not simultaneously available for the same row system, the result remains a direct protein-omics association rather than a translation-mediated claim. If all controlled $S^{QP}_{ik}$ entries were nonpositive, the $B^{\ast}_{Q6}$ block would remain only at the code-read or translation-boundary level for that protein-omics readout. In the present recorded protein-abundance rows, the direct $Q \to P$ check is positive in all three organisms, but the measured $Q \to T \to P$ mediation check is still not supplied by these data.

\chapter{Translation-Mediated Protein-Function Boundary for $B^{*}_{Q6}$ Residuals}
\label{ch:q6-translation-mediated-protein-function-boundary-cross-layer}
\origin{ai}

\subsection{Finite Row Carrier and Layer Order}
\label{sec:q6-translation-protein-function-finite-carrier}

\paragraph{Carrier.}
The finite carrier for this record is a matrix of cells $(q_i,T_j,P_k,F_l)$.  The coordinate $q_i$ is one of the amino-acid-quotient residual coordinates in $B^{*}_{Q6}$, evaluated only after amino-acid composition has been removed from the codon signal.  The symbol $T_j$ denotes a translation readout boundary, such as a measured translation-efficiency row or a bounded codon-adaptation readback.  The symbol $P_k$ denotes a protein-omics intermediary, such as matched protein abundance.  The symbol $F_l$ is not populated by the present data unless a separate function or phenotype assay is later attached.  Thus the row order is not $q_i \to F_l$.  The permitted order is first $q_i \to T_j$, then $q_i \to T_j \to P_k$, and only after a distinct higher-layer contact may a row be tested against $F_l$.

\paragraph{Residual coordinates.}
The computed coordinate set has cardinality $9$:
$$
\begin{aligned}
R_{Q6}=\{&K\_AAA,\ Arg\_AGR,\ Ile\_AUA,\ Leu\_CUN\_vs\_UUR,\ Leu\_UUA\_vs\_UUG,\\
&Ser\_UCR\_vs\_AGY,\ Ser\_UCA\_vs\_UCG,\ Thr\_ACR\_vs\_ACY,\ f3\_stress\}.
\end{aligned}
$$
These coordinates were imported from the $B^{*}_{Q6}$ translation-survival computation and are treated as residual directions rather than as biological measurements.  Their finite role is to index controlled tests; they do not themselves observe ribosomes, folded proteins, physical feasibility, molecular activity, phenotype, or a universal rule.

\paragraph{Controls.}
The verified design uses an intercept, $\log(\mathrm{cds\_len\_nt})$, the $20$ amino-acid composition proportions $A,C,D,E,F,G,H,I,K,L,M,N,P,Q,R,S,T,V,W,Y$, $GC3$, an $M$-density baseline, and $\log_{10}(\mathrm{abundance\_ppm})$ as a measured protein-abundance control.  The abundance term is part of the control surface for this finite row: a residual association remaining after this adjustment is a beyond-expression association, not a mechanism.  The positive threshold reported for partial $R^2$ is $10^{-12}$.

\subsection{Powered Essentiality Rows}
\label{sec:q6-translation-protein-function-essentiality-rows}

\paragraph{Escherichia coli row.}
For \emph{Escherichia coli} K12 MG1655, the essentiality-protein-abundance join reports $3739$ abundance-joined records, with $3737$ genes used.  The row contains $285$ essential genes and $3452$ nonessential genes, giving essential fraction $0.07626438319507627$.  The controlled design has $25$ control columns, $\operatorname{rank}(Z)=24$, $9$ residual coordinates, and $\operatorname{rank}(\widetilde X)=9$.  The residual degrees of freedom after $Z$ are $3713$.  The residual response energy is $232.13814987247684$, and the explained energy attributed to the residual coordinate block is $2.243840654793728$.  The reported finite essentiality association is
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^2_{FE}(\mathrm{E\ coli}) = 0.00966597112980509.
\end{aligned}
$$
All $9$ residual coordinates have positive finite entries under the stated threshold in this essentiality readback.

\paragraph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae row.}
For \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}, the essentiality-protein-abundance join reports $5815$ abundance-joined records, with $5511$ genes used.  The row contains $1168$ essential genes and $4343$ nonessential genes, giving essential fraction $0.2119397568499365$.  The controlled design again has $25$ control columns, $\operatorname{rank}(Z)=24$, $9$ residual coordinates, and $\operatorname{rank}(\widetilde X)=9$.  The residual degrees of freedom after $Z$ are $5487$.  The residual response energy is $826.488384003322$, and the explained energy attributed to the residual coordinate block is $2.741892921952373$.  The reported finite essentiality association is
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^2_{FE}(\mathrm{S\ cerevisiae}) = 0.003317521425614316.
\end{aligned}
$$
Again, all $9$ residual coordinates have positive finite entries under the stated threshold in this essentiality readback.

\paragraph{Rank condition.}
Both organism rows are rank-powered for the declared residual coordinate block.  In each case $\operatorname{rank}(\widetilde X)=9$ equals the number of tested residual coordinates, while $\operatorname{rank}(Z)=24$ under $25$ listed control columns.  The degeneracy cutoff recorded for the controlled surface is $0.999999$.  These numbers support the narrow claim that the finite residual block is estimable after the listed controls in the two reported organism rows.  They do not support an organism-general law, because the matrix is still only a two-organism powered essentiality surface for the verified facts presented here.

\subsection{Mediation Gate Before Function}
\label{sec:q6-translation-protein-function-mediation-gate}

\paragraph{Four cases.}
For a fixed coordinate $q_i$, translation readout $T_j$, and protein-omics readout $P_k$, the readback is partitioned into four finite cases.  Case A is no survival: neither the translation surface nor the protein-omics surface gives a positive controlled residual entry.  Case B is translation-only survival: $q_i$ survives against $T_j$, but the downstream protein-omics surface is absent or null.  Case C is protein-omics survival not mediated by the measured translation row: $q_i$ is associated with $P_k$, but the $q_i \to T_j$ step is absent or conditioning on $T_j$ does not account for the direct protein-omics term.  Case D is translation-mediated protein-omics survival: $q_i \to T_j$ survives and the direct $q_i \to P_k$ contribution is reduced or absorbed after conditioning on $T_j$.

\paragraph{Boundary equation.}
The admissible finite readback can be summarized as a gated relation, not as a causal assertion:
$$
\begin{aligned}
\mathrm{carry}(q_i,F_l)=1
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
&\mathrm{survive}(q_i,T_j)=1,\\
&\mathrm{mediate}(q_i,T_j,P_k)=1,\\
&\mathrm{contact}(F_l)=1.
\end{aligned}
$$
The present verified facts contribute controlled essentiality associations after amino-acid composition and measured protein-abundance controls.  They do not supply the missing functional contact $\mathrm{contact}(F_l)$.  Therefore the function row remains empty even when a finite association with an essentiality phenotype is reported.  Essentiality here is a laboratory knockout binary phenotype joined to codon counts and abundance; it is not a synonymous-edit experiment, not a translation-mechanism assay, and not a molecular-function measurement.

\paragraph{Essentiality surface.}
The verified readout is a binary linear-probability residualization for lab knockout essentiality.  Its two recorded values, $0.00966597112980509$ for \emph{E. coli} and $0.003317521425614316$ for \emph{S. cerevisiae}, are small positive controlled associations after abundance adjustment.  Because protein abundance is already included as a confounding control, these values are not evidence that translation mediates abundance, nor evidence that abundance mediates function.  They are finite row entries at the cross-layer boundary: residual codon coordinates retain a small association with the binary phenotype after the stated controls.

\subsection{Local No-Promotion Rule}
\label{sec:q6-translation-protein-function-no-promotion}

\paragraph{Translation.}
A modeled tAI or stAI weight is allowed only as an organism-scoped codon-adaptation readback.  It is not a ribosome-profiling observation.  A ribosome-profiling or translation-efficiency contact would be needed to test measured translation survival.  Matched mRNA abundance is only a control for separating transcript abundance from translation efficiency; transcript counts alone do not realize translation.

\paragraph{Protein and function.}
Matched proteomics may provide a protein-abundance intermediary, but steady-state abundance is not folding, localization, stability, molecular function, organismal phenotype, or mechanism.  A direct $q_i \to P_k$ association cannot be promoted to translation-mediated protein readback unless the same coordinate first survives against $T_j$ and the $T_j$ conditioning reduces the direct protein-omics contribution.  Even a positive $q_i \to T_j \to P_k$ row stops before function unless a separate assay supplies $F_l$.

\paragraph{Cannot-claim boundary.}
From the rows recorded here, no translation realization, ribosome mechanism, folded-structure claim, physical-admissibility claim, molecular-function claim, phenotype-realization claim, causal pathway claim, or universal biological law follows.  Each of those would require a layer-matched contact and a separately recorded statistic.  The current chapter records a finite cross-layer boundary: $B^{*}_{Q6}$ residual coordinates can be tested through controlled translation and protein-omics intermediates, while function remains null until a direct function or phenotype readout is attached.

\subsection{Verdict for the Standalone Record}
\label{sec:q6-translation-protein-function-verdict}

\paragraph{Ready statement.}
The standalone record is ready as a bounded BioReality chapter because it has a finite residual coordinate list, named control surfaces, two powered organism rows, concrete ranks, concrete row counts, and measured finite $R^2_{FE}$ values.  The chapter's claim is not that synonymous residual geometry determines biological function.  The claim is that any route from $B^{*}_{Q6}$ residuals toward protein-function language must pass through a translation readout and then a protein-omics mediation test before a higher-layer function row can be considered.  In the present data, the function row is intentionally unfilled.

\subsection{Rank-Powered Modeled Translation Survival for B${}^\ast_{Q6}$ Residual Coordinates}\label{sec:q6-rank-powered-translation-survival-cross-layer}\origin{ai}

\paragraph{Finite carrier and residual coordinate surface.} The finite object considered here is an organism-by-CDS readout surface in which each B${}^\ast_{Q6}$ synonymous coordinate is first separated from amino-acid identity and then compared with a bounded translation-weight readout. The active coordinate list has nine entries: $K\_AAA$, $Arg\_AGR$, $Ile\_AUA$, $Leu\_CUN\_vs\_UUR$, $Leu\_UUA\_vs\_UUG$, $Ser\_UCR\_vs\_AGY$, $Ser\_UCA\_vs\_UCG$, $Thr\_ACR\_vs\_ACY$, and $f3\_stress$. These coordinates are not treated as raw codon preferences. Each coordinate $q_i$ is read only through its quotient residual $q_i-P_\tau q_i$, where $P_\tau$ denotes the projection onto amino-acid-sequence information. The biological question is therefore not whether a codon label is frequent, but whether the remaining synonymous residue has any controlled survival against a translation-weight surface after ordinary composition and organism-level effects have been removed.

\paragraph{Powered modeled-tAI panel.} The powered finite panel contains $40$ organisms and computes a modeled per-codon tAI readout from GtRNAdb anticodon copy counts together with dos Reis wobble weights. The modeled readout is projected onto the same nine B${}^\ast_{Q6}$ residual coordinates, producing a $9\times 9$ $S^{QT}$ surface. The control design contains $24$ control columns, with $\operatorname{rank}(Z)=23$ and residual degrees of freedom $17$ after removing amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, CDS length, M-density, and simpler codon-usage baselines. The residualized coordinate block has $\operatorname{rank}(\widetilde X)=9$, so the panel is not in the trivial all-fitted regime. The recorded aggregate statistic is
$$
\begin{aligned}
R^2_{QT} &= 0.9003849000036579,\\
\operatorname{rank}(Z) &= 23,\\
\operatorname{rank}(\widetilde X) &= 9,\\
\operatorname{df}_{\mathrm{resid}} &= 17.
\end{aligned}
$$
The positive-entry threshold used for partial-$R^2$ survival is $10^{-12}$, while the rank-degenerate rejection threshold is $0.999999$. Because $R^2_{QT}=0.9003849000036579$ is below the degeneracy cutoff and the residualized coordinate rank is full for the nine-coordinate block, this record is admissible as a powered modeled-readout survival surface rather than as a saturated projection artifact.

\paragraph{Rank artifact excluded by comparison.} The earlier three-organism modeled-tAI computation is retained only as a boundary case. It used the same nine synonymous residual coordinates and produced nonzero bounded coupling across $81$ entries above threshold $0.01$, with maximum absolute entry $3801961.031607896$. However, that three-organism surface is not a powered survival result. With $n=3$, any all-$1.0$ controlled survival pattern is a rank-saturation null boundary rather than biological evidence. The larger panel changes the status of the calculation because it supplies $40$ organism rows, a residual control rank of $23$, and $17$ residual degrees of freedom after controls. Thus the powered record may support a modeled translation-weight relation, while the three-organism record supplies only a warning about rank saturation and bounded coupling.

\paragraph{Readout interpretation.} The supported statement is deliberately narrow. At least one entry of the controlled $S^{QT}$ matrix survives as a positive modeled translation-readout relation between a B${}^\ast_{Q6}$ amino-acid-quotient residual coordinate and a modeled tAI coordinate under the declared controls. The readout is organism-level codon adaptation inferred from tRNA gene-copy annotations and wobble weights. It is not ribosome profiling, not measured elongation speed, not ribosome occupancy, not proteomics, and not a direct protein-realization measurement. The finite row record therefore licenses only a modeled translation-weight survival statement: B${}^\ast_{Q6}$ is no longer merely a code-read or bounded codon-count residual basis for this modeled tAI layer, because the powered controlled surface remains positive without rank saturation.

\paragraph{Local non-promotion rule.} No stronger biological layer follows from this chapter. A modeled $S^{QT}$ entry does not establish measured translation output, protein abundance, folding, structural order, physical admissibility, molecular function, phenotype, mechanism, or universality. Any one of those conclusions would require a separate row-matched contact at that layer, with its own controls and its own statistic. In particular, modeled tAI or stAI weights must not be read as ribosome occupancy; a positive $S^{QT}$ entry must not be promoted to $S^{QP}$ or to a mediated $M^{QTP}$ claim; and no single coordinate, including the Leu CUN/UUR split, becomes a population-level translation law from this record alone. The break condition for this chapter would be met if every controlled $S^{QT}_{ij}$ entry were nonpositive after the declared baselines, or if the apparent signal came only from a rank-saturated design. The present finite panel avoids the latter failure and records a positive modeled-readout survival surface, while leaving measured translation and higher biological layers outside the claim.

\subsection{q6.rank-powered.translation-conditioned.protein-mediation.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested on a rank-powered matched panel containing codon usage, tAI or stAI translation readouts, ribosome-profiling translation efficiency, matched mRNA abundance, and matched protein abundance per CDS

\paragraph{Current claim.} For a rank-powered matched organism panel, at least one B*\_Q6 residual coordinate has positive translation-readout survival S\textasciicircum{}QT and positive protein-abundance survival S\textasciicircum{}QP, and the corresponding direct q\_i -> P\_k signal is absorbed by a measured translation readout T\_j after mRNA and amino-acid-composition controls; otherwise B*\_Q6 remains bounded to translation readout or direct protein-omics prediction without mechanism.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-proteomics-protein-abundance}. per-protein abundance estimates (e.g. PAXdb integrated rank or ppm) for the same organism as the codon-usage and ribo-seq contacts

Can test: downstream mediation chain Q\_i -> T\_j (translation readout) -> P\_k (protein abundance) once both translation and protein contacts are attached. Cannot test: folding pathway dynamics; function realization; physical admissibility from steady-state abundance alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite matrix surface over B*\_Q6 residual coordinates q\_i, matched translation readouts T\_j, matched protein-abundance readouts P\_k, and transcript controls per CDS across a rank-powered organism panel. Distinctions: translation-surviving versus translation-null residual coordinate; protein-omics-surviving versus protein-omics-null residual coordinate; mediated q\_i -> T\_j -> P\_k signal versus direct or confounded q\_i -> P\_k signal. Readback: per-entry four-case readback A/B/C/D after residualizing Q, T, and P against amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and matched mRNA controls. Internal structure: coordinate; rank; relation.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\textbf{residual-basis.m-only-local-optimum-insufficiency}. If M-only is the true BEDC variable for DNA-to-protein, then for every organism and every readout in \{tAI, ribosome occupancy, protein abundance\}, adding any element of B*\_Q6 to an M-density baseline (with amino-acid composition controlled) does not increase the BEDC survival score. Equivalently, S\_Q(r | Z, Delta\_M) <= 0 for every Q in B*\_Q6.

Support condition: Codon-usage tables for at least 3 organisms with tRNA-Leu, tRNA-Arg, tRNA-Ile, tRNA-Ser, tRNA-Thr copy-number data (e.g. Kazusa + GtRNAdb), plus a readout source (stAI or ribosome-profiling-derived efficiency).

Break condition: Some Q in B*\_Q6 has S\_Q(r | Z, Delta\_M) > 0 in at least one organism on at least one readout; or CUN/UUR Leu log-ratio shows sign reversal between organisms after amino-acid composition control while Delta\_M does not.

\subsection{q6.translation-mediated.system-phenotype-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested against matched translation readouts before any downstream system phenotype readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} If a rank-powered matched panel supplies B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, translation readouts, mRNA controls, and phenotype labels on the same CDS or organism surface, then any admissible Q6-to-phenotype frontier claim must decompose through translation first: q\_i may enter the system\_phenotype layer only as q\_i -> T\_j -> phenotype\_k, with the direct phenotype association rejected as a boundary artifact whenever the controlled translation leg is absent, rank-degenerate, or erased by baseline ablation.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite matched CDS-by-organism matrix carrying B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, translation readouts, and system phenotype labels on the same readout surface. Distinctions: amino-acid-quotient-surviving codon residual q\_i is separated from amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls; translation readout T\_j is explicitly measured or externally supplied before any phenotype readout is tested; phenotype association is admitted only after conditioning on the mandatory translation readout. Readback: per-entry gated relation q\_i -> T\_j -> phenotype\_k with break\_condition, AA-composition control, simpler-baseline ablation, and refusal of phenotype interpretation when the translation leg is absent or rank-degenerate. Internal structure: relation; rank; closure.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.physical-admissibility-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory translation readouts before any downstream physical-admissibility readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a physical-admissibility boundary only through a decomposition that first tests translation-readout survival and then asks whether any downstream physical-admissibility association remains or is attenuated after conditioning on the translation readout.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite matched CDS panel carrying B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates, translation readout surfaces, optional protein-omics readbacks, and a future physical-admissibility readout surface. Distinctions: q\_i is amino-acid-quotient residualized before any readout test; T\_j is a measured or modeled translation readout, not a protein mechanism by itself; A physical-admissibility signal may be interpreted only after q\_i -> T\_j survival or an explicit conditioned boundary test. Readback: conditioned cross-layer table separating q\_i -> T\_j survival, q\_i -> physical-admissibility residual association, and translation-conditioned attenuation. Internal structure: coordinate; relation; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.post-translational-state-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory translation readouts before any downstream post-translational protein-state readout such as PTM, localization, or stability is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a post-translational protein-state boundary only through the gated decomposition q\_i -> T\_j -> P\_k: first translation-readout survival must be established under amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls, and only then may a matched PTM, localization, or stability readout be tested for mediation rather than direct codon-topology association.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite B*\_Q6 residual-coordinate by translation-readout by post-translational protein-state readout surface over matched CDS panels. Distinctions: q\_i residual coordinate survives amino-acid quotient before any protein-state interpretation; translation readout T\_j is measured or modeled separately from protein-state readout P\_k; conditioning on T\_j must reduce any direct q\_i to P\_k association before a translation-mediated protein-state claim is allowed. Readback: per-entry discriminant for q\_i -> T\_j -> P\_k with null, translation-only, direct protein-state, and mediated cases reported separately. Internal structure: coordinate; relation; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-stability-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through mandatory translation readouts before any downstream protein-stability or degradation-rate readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a protein-stability boundary only through a rank-powered matched panel where q\_i first survives a translation readout T\_j under controls, and any downstream stability or degradation readout is reported as translation-conditioned mediation rather than direct codon-topology mechanism.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-proteomics-protein-abundance}. per-protein abundance estimates (e.g. PAXdb integrated rank or ppm) for the same organism as the codon-usage and ribo-seq contacts

Can test: downstream mediation chain Q\_i -> T\_j (translation readout) -> P\_k (protein abundance) once both translation and protein contacts are attached. Cannot test: folding pathway dynamics; function realization; physical admissibility from steady-state abundance alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite organism-by-CDS panel carrying B*\_Q6 residual coordinates, matched translation readouts, matched mRNA controls, and protein-stability readout surface where available. Distinctions: each B*\_Q6 coordinate is first amino-acid-quotient residualized; translation readout T\_j must survive controls before any protein-stability readout is interpreted; protein-stability signal is reported only as translation-conditioned residual association, not folding mechanism or phenotype. Readback: per-entry gated matrix q\_i -> T\_j -> protein-stability readout with AA-composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, and mRNA controls. Internal structure: relation; rank; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite CDS-indexed surface whose coordinates are B*\_Q6 synonymous residuals, matched translation readouts, mRNA controls, and a downstream protein-localization readout slot. Distinctions: q\_i residual coordinate survives the amino-acid quotient before any higher-layer readout is tested; T\_j translation readout is measured or modeled separately from the downstream protein-localization readout; localization interpretation is refused unless q\_i first survives or is conditioned through a translation-readout boundary. Readback: read back only the gated relation q\_i -> T\_j -> localization\_k, with a null boundary when matched localization contacts are absent. Internal structure: coordinate; relation; trigger.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\subsection{q6.n3-rank-degenerate.translation-survival-refusal.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates tested against modeled tAI translation readouts across the current three-organism panel under amino-acid composition, GC3, length, and M-density controls

\paragraph{Current claim.} If the controlled B*\_Q6 to modeled-tAI survival matrix is computed on only the current three organisms, the resulting all-1.0 fit must be classified as rank saturation rather than biological survival; the admissible next step is a rank-powered translation-readout panel, not downstream interpretation.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{trna-gene-copy-numbers}. External genome annotation resources report organism-level tRNA-Leu gene-copy counts by anticodon family.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; tRNA-Leu gene-copy spectrum. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite 3-organism B*\_Q6 residual-coordinate by modeled-tAI readout surface with control-residualized row space. Distinctions: rank-degenerate all-1.0 survival output is separated from powered translation-readout survival; B*\_Q6 residual coordinates remain translation-readout candidates rather than accepted survival coordinates at n=3. Readback: reject any S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\} pass call when organism count and residual degrees of freedom make the controlled projection saturated. Internal structure: rank; relation.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\paragraph{Mismatch ledger.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion.scope-review}. Status: aligned. \subsection{Translation-Conditioned Four-Case Survival Discriminant}
\label{sec:q6-translation-conditioned-four-case-survival-discriminant}

\paragraph{Finite carrier.}
This record studies a finite matched-row matrix whose columns separate three kinds of quantities: residual coordinates $q_i$ from the $B^*_{Q6}$ coding-sequence basis, bounded translation readouts $T_j$, and matched protein-omics readouts $P_k$.  The row universe is not an abstract population law.  It is the finite joined panel present in the run: $3739$ joined coding-sequence and protein rows for \emph{Escherichia coli} K-12 MG1655, using the \emph{E. coli} tRNA-copy bridge, and $5815$ joined rows for \emph{Saccharomyces cerevisiae}, using the yeast tRNA-copy bridge.  The translation readout is the per-protein mean modeled tAI,
$$\begin{aligned}
T &= \frac{\sum_{c} \operatorname{count}(c)\operatorname{tAI}(c)}{\sum_{c}\operatorname{count}(c)},
\end{aligned}$$
formed from codon counts, GtRNAdb all-tRNA gene-copy counts, and dos Reis wobble weights.  The protein-omics readout is $\log_{10}(\operatorname{abundance\_ppm})$ from measured PAXdb protein abundance after joining to the real coding-sequence codon table.  Thus the carrier is finite, organism-indexed, and row-matched, but the translation side is explicitly a modeled adaptation readout rather than ribosome-profiling evidence.

\paragraph{Controlled residualization and rank gate.}
Before any positive call is allowed, the $q_i$ matrix is residualized against the declared control block $Z$, including amino-acid composition, organism-level context inside the matched panel, GC3, coding-sequence length, M-density, mRNA-related controls when present, and simpler baseline codon summaries.  In the verified finite run, the control block has $24$ columns and numerical rank $23$ in both organisms.  After residualization, the \emph{E. coli} panel has $3716$ residual degrees of freedom, residual translation energy $0.4728468725814029$, residual protein-abundance energy $4020.333671802061$, and full residual $q$-rank $9$ for both the $T$ and $P$ regressions.  The yeast panel has $5792$ residual degrees of freedom, residual translation energy $6.90537888244409$, residual protein-abundance energy $2958.1865890925337$, and again full residual $q$-rank $9$ for both readouts.  The degenerate-fit refusal threshold is $R^2=0.999999$, and both organism blocks are recorded as rank-sufficient.  This rank condition is part of the scientific content: a saturated or rank-collapsed surface is treated as a null boundary rather than as positive survival.

\paragraph{Discriminant.}
For each residual coordinate and readout pair, the finite statistic has three surfaces: $S^{QT}$ for the controlled coordinate-to-translation association, $S^{QP}$ for the controlled coordinate-to-protein association, and $M^{QTP}$ for the reduction of the direct coordinate-to-protein association after conditioning on the translation readout.  The case map is
$$\begin{aligned}
A &: S^{QT}\leq 0\ \text{and}\ S^{QP}\leq 0,\\
B &: S^{QT}>0\ \text{and}\ S^{QP}\leq 0,\\
C &: S^{QT}\leq 0\ \text{and}\ S^{QP}>0,\\
D &: S^{QT}>0\ \text{and}\ S^{QP}>0\ \text{with a translation-conditioning drop in the direct } q_i\to P_k \text{ term}.
\end{aligned}$$
The classification threshold used for the sign and mediation decisions is $10^{-12}$.  The aggregate controlled fits in the finite run are $R^2_{QT}=0.5416736993691318$ and $R^2_{QP}=0.10767526677985896$ for \emph{E. coli}, and $R^2_{QT}=0.8213457220900503$ and $R^2_{QP}=0.29837624994140827$ for yeast, each over $9$ residual coordinates.  These values describe the finite controlled matrices; they are not promoted to a universal codon law.

\paragraph{Finite readback.}
The case counts are organism-specific.  In \emph{E. coli}, no coordinate falls in case $A$, $B$, or $C$; $7$ coordinates fall in case $D$, and $2$ positive-positive coordinates are recorded without the required mediation drop.  There are no unclassified coordinates.  In yeast, no coordinate falls in case $A$, $B$, or $C$; all $9$ coordinates fall in case $D$, with no positive-positive coordinate left outside the mediation-drop condition.  Equivalently, the finite readback is a two-organism, rank-powered, controlled four-case assignment: $7/9$ mediated-propagation calls plus $2/9$ positive-positive non-drop boundary calls in \emph{E. coli}, and $9/9$ mediated-propagation calls in yeast.  The protein-side explained energy carried by the residual coordinate block is $432.89050065533684$ for \emph{E. coli} and $882.6526210803958$ for yeast; the corresponding translation-side explained energies are $0.256128714706293$ and $5.671703404506426$.

\paragraph{Scientific scope.}
The chapter records an objective four-case classification over controlled finite matrices.  It does not by itself establish observed ribosome occupancy, causal translation dynamics, protein folding, physical admissibility, molecular function, phenotype, perturbation response, or a universal biological law.  Case $C$, if it appears in a future panel, must be read only as a protein-omics predictive residual not mediated by the measured $T_j$ readout; it is not a translation explanation.  Case $D$ is likewise a translation-conditioned propagation label inside this finite design, not a causal mechanism claim.  The modeled tAI readout is bounded by its construction from tRNA gene-copy and wobble weights; measured ribosome-profiling or translation-efficiency rows would be a separate contact.  PAXdb abundance is a steady-state protein-omics readout and is not isolated from mRNA abundance, turnover, localization, post-translational modification, folding, or pathway context.  Any claim about real translation dynamics, folding, physical admissibility, biological function, mechanistic intervention, phenotype, or universality requires an additional layer-matched contact and its own controlled statistic.

\subsection{q6.rank-admissible.translation-survival-gate.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates across a rank-audited multi-organism panel with codon usage, tRNA gene-copy or tAI/stAI translation readouts, and controls for amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density

\paragraph{Current claim.} Before any B*\_Q6 coordinate is promoted from codon-topology residual to translation-realization survival, each proposed S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\}\_\{ij\} entry must pass a rank-admissibility gate: after residualizing q\_i and translation readout T\_j against Z, the remaining design must have non-saturated rank and enough independent organism/readout support to make held-out survival falsifiable. If the gate fails, the entry remains coupling-only or needs\_data even when the fitted in-sample score is positive or equal to 1.0.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{trna-gene-copy-numbers}. External genome annotation resources report organism-level tRNA-Leu gene-copy counts by anticodon family.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; tRNA-Leu gene-copy spectrum. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite residualized design matrix over B*\_Q6 coordinates, organism-indexed translation readout surfaces, and control columns Z. Distinctions: rank-admissible translation-survival entry versus rank-saturated projection artifact; coupling-only contact versus controlled survival contact; translation-readout boundary before any protein, structure, function, or phenotype interpretation. Readback: per-entry admissibility certificate for S\textasciicircum{}\{QT\}\_\{ij\}: residualized rank, degrees of freedom after Z, held-out translation readout boundary, and break condition for rejecting survival when rank is saturated. Internal structure: rank; relation; closure.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout

\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\subsection{q6.translation-conditioned.protein-interaction-boundary.cross-layer}

\paragraph{Biological object.} B*\_Q6 synonymous residual coordinates in coding sequences tested through matched translation readouts and protein-abundance controls before any downstream protein-interaction or complex-membership readout is interpreted

\paragraph{Current claim.} A B*\_Q6 residual coordinate may enter a protein-interaction or complex-membership readout only through a gated decomposition that first demonstrates rank-powered translation-readout survival and then tests whether conditioning on matched translation readout and protein abundance removes the direct q\_i association with the interaction readout.

\paragraph{External reality input.}
\textbf{codon-usage-per-organism}. External per-organism codon-usage resources report codon-count or frequency summaries for coding sequences.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon usage distribution. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{tai-or-stai-weights}. External tAI or stAI resources provide organism-scoped codon adaptation weights that can be used as a bounded translation-layer readout when kept separate from BEDC coordinate geometry.

Can test: translation\_realization; cross\_layer\_relation; codon adaptation weight readback. Cannot test: folded structure; physical admissibility; function realization; global biological law.

\textbf{ribosome-profiling-translation-efficiency}. per-CDS ribosome occupancy or translation efficiency in organism-matched ribo-seq experiments, paired with mRNA abundance to compute TE = RPF / mRNA

Can test: B*\_Q6 residual coordinate survival on translation efficiency under amino-acid composition + GC3 + length + M-density baseline controls. Cannot test: protein folding, mature protein abundance, function, mechanism, or universality from ribo-seq alone.

\textbf{matched-mrna-abundance-control}. per-CDS mRNA abundance in the same experimental condition as the ribo-seq run, enabling TE = RPF / mRNA computation as a separated control

Can test: isolation of translation-layer signal from transcription-layer signal via TE residual. Cannot test: translation realization from transcript abundance alone; CUN/UUR versus tRNA-Leu association without matched codon-usage and tRNA-copy contacts; protein folding, physical admissibility, function, or universality from transcript counts alone.

\textbf{matched-proteomics-protein-abundance}. per-protein abundance estimates (e.g. PAXdb integrated rank or ppm) for the same organism as the codon-usage and ribo-seq contacts

Can test: downstream mediation chain Q\_i -> T\_j (translation readout) -> P\_k (protein abundance) once both translation and protein contacts are attached. Cannot test: folding pathway dynamics; function realization; physical admissibility from steady-state abundance alone.

\paragraph{Internal newmath/BEDC derivation.} Carrier: finite panel of coding sequences indexed by organism, B*\_Q6 residual coordinate, mandatory translation readout, protein-abundance control, and downstream protein-interaction readout surface. Distinctions: q\_i residual coordinate survives amino-acid quotient control or is compressed away; translation readout T\_j mediates or fails to mediate the q\_i to protein-interaction readout association; protein-interaction readout is interpreted only after translation-readout conditioning. Readback: per-entry gated decomposition q\_i -> T\_j -> protein-interaction readout with AA composition, organism, GC3, length, M-density, mRNA, and protein-abundance controls. Internal structure: relation; rank; closure.

\paragraph{Falsifiable probes.}
\textbf{residual-basis.translation-readout-boundary.no-promotion}. The residual-basis packet may use an external tAI or stAI weight only as a bounded translation-readout contact; any claim about ribosome occupancy, protein abundance, folded structure, physical admissibility, function, or a global law requires a separate reality contact at that layer.

Support condition: The packet already has codon-usage, tRNA-copy, and tAI or stAI contacts, and it keeps BEDC residual coordinates separate from the external readout source.

Break condition: The packet treats B*\_Q6 geometry, S\_Q notation, tAI or stAI weights, or organism-level codon tables as direct evidence of ribosome dynamics, protein output, folded structure, physical admissibility, biological function, or universal biological law.

\textbf{b-star-q6-survival-matrix-break-condition}. For every (q\_i, r\_j) pair in B*\_Q6 x translation readouts and every organism in the contact, compute S\_ij with full Z controls. Survival is supported if at least one (i, j) entry is strictly positive (S\_ij > 0) after baseline ablation.

Support condition: At least one (q\_i, r\_j) entry achieves S\_ij > 0 with partial R\textasciicircum{}2 above the agreed threshold under all Z controls and simpler-codon-baseline ablation

Break condition: All (q\_i, r\_j) survival scores S\_ij <= 0 after amino-acid composition, organism, GC3, length, and M-density baseline controls; equivalently, no B*\_Q6 coordinate improves the minimum description length of any translation readout
